    "Is turnover (~300 births/deaths per 600s) fast enough to detect selection on alpha within a single run, using inherit_alpha_corr and lumen_end_mean_alpha to test whether H-024's inheritance-fidelity claim is measurable before we touch MutationSigma?"
  ],
  "rationale": "All three open questions are population-level stability/turnover questions, not single-organism claims, so they fit the lab's mandate directly. Priority 1 comes first because every downstream card (H-021, H-023, H-024) implicitly assumes the regen-6 band is reproducible; running it only for one seed leaves every other conjecture standing on an unverified baseline. Priority 2 is next because H-014 is still unmet and H-021 is an open conjecture about what sets the Lumen floor \u2014 a controlled drought run can move both cards from conjecture toward supported/refuted using lumen_min_n and lumen_end_n rather than a single anecdotal drop. Priority 3 operationalizes H-024 (mutation/inheritance fidelity) and extends the partial support already logged for H-013, using inherit_alpha_corr and lumen_max_generation to check whether the run window is even long enough to see selection before we spend effort widening MutationSigma. A flawed-but-executed sweep on any of these beats another cycle of conjecture without numbers; the lab leaves this meeting with three concrete runs to launch, not three more hypotheses to admire."
}
```

  "rationale": "Metric substitution required: docketed crux names lumen_min_n, but H-025 rules it out \u2014 min_n is the opening-seconds trough (X-020: both arms hit exactly 40.0 regardless of treatment) and cannot test a foraging/regen-coupling claim that plays out over a run. lumen_end_n is the allowed-list proxy for a sustained population floor and is what H-027's \"floor... balancing learned foraging efficiency\" actually describes.\n\nMode = B (learning on, evolution off) in both arms: isolates the lifetime-learning-regen coupling H-027 claims without letting Gaussian-mutation genome drift (mode C) supply an alternate explanation for any floor shift \u2014 keeps the test decisive on one mechanism at a time.\n\nbLeviathan=false in both arms: H-027 explicitly claims \"not predation\" \u2014 holding predation off in both control and treatment removes it as a confound so any floor shift is attributable to the regen/learning axis being tested, not a predation-rate artifact.\n\nDesign: 2-arm, regen-6 (control) vs regen-3 (treatment/drought), seeds 1-2, 600s, else defaults. If treatment lumen_end_n is within 8 of control (or higher) despite halved regen, that supports H-027 (floor decoupled from proportional resource cut, consistent with learned-foraging buffering). A drop \u22658 below control indicates simple resource-scarcity crash, falsifying the coupling claim. Two seeds, one factor changed (PatchRegenPerSec), effect size read directly off lumen_end_n \u2014 cheapest arm that can discriminate crash-vs-buffer without conflating evolution or predation. A mode-A-vs-B follow-up (learning off vs on, both at regen-3) is the next cheapest step if this arm confirms decoupling, to attribute the buffering specifically to learning rather than population dynamics generally \u2014 not run here to keep this round's spend minimal."
}
```

If the analysis script keys off the literal field name rather than the prose rule, a genuinely supportive result (treatment slightly below but within the 8-unit band) could be misclassified as non-confirmatory.     "Before anyone writes 'the floor is identical/decoupled,' quote the actual lumen_end_n values for both arms across both seeds. Don't let the threshold language launder a real difference into 'no effect.'",
    "Single-timepoint metric: lumen_end_n is one snapshot at t=600, not a sustained floor. Alternative explanation for any 'within 8' result: the population is still oscillating and t=600 caught both arms mid-swing in the same phase by coincidence, not because a floor has stabilized. Report a trailing-window mean/variance (e.g., last 60\u2013120s) alongside the single endpoint, or the 'floor' claim isn't tested, only a coin-flip timestamp is.",
    "Two seeds is the bare minimum, not a law-grade base. State per-seed values separately, not just an aggregate, before drawing the coupling/crash conclusion.",
